## Document 003: 10月加息定价过头了？瑞银：核心PCE下修叠加基数效应，美联储不太可能连续加息

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月加息定价过头了？瑞银：核心PCE下修叠加基数效应，美联储不太可能连续加息.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-25
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

瑞银认为，美联储政策收紧的实际幅度很可能低于当前市场定价所暗⽰的⽔平，其基准情
景为 12 ⽉再加息⼀次后维持利率不变，同时预计核⼼ PCE 通胀将在年度修订中下调 0.2 个百
分点，叠加明年上半年有利的基数效应，连续⼤幅加息的基础正在削弱。
摘要：
美国国债收益率急升引发市场震荡，但瑞银认为当前市场对美联储紧缩路径的定价过于激进，
政策收紧⼒度或不及预期，整体投资前景依然具有建设性。
近⽇， 10 年期美国国债收益率单⽇上涨近 15 个基点，突破 5.1% ，创 2007 年以来新⾼，标普 500
指数下跌 0.8% 。这⼀波动由油价上涨、强劲经济数据以及疲弱的国债拍卖结果共同驱动，联邦
基⾦期货隐含的 10 ⽉加息概率随之升⾄ 70% ，较⼀周前不⾜ 50% 的⽔平⼤幅跳升。
据追⻛交易台消息，瑞银⾸席投资官 Mark Haefele 及其团队在最新报告中指出，美联储政策收
紧的实际幅度很可能低于当前市场定价所暗⽰的⽔平。瑞银基准情景为美联储于 12 ⽉再加息⼀
次后维持利率不变，同时预计核⼼ PCE 通胀将于本⽉经由美国经济分析局年度修订下调 0.2 个百

分点，叠加明年上半年有利的基数效应，连续⼤幅加息的基础正在削弱。
加息预期或⾼估， PCE 修订成关键变量
瑞银认为，尽管近期美联储官员的表态偏鹰派，但市场对紧缩路径的定价已超出基本⾯所能⽀
撑的程度。报告指出，美联储政策制定者的利率预测中值显⽰ 2027 年全年维持利率不变，⽽核
⼼ PCE 通胀的向下修订将进⼀步压缩连续加息的空间。
具体来看，美国经济分析局即将进⾏的年度修订预计将把核⼼ PCE 通胀下调 0.2 个百分点，这⼀
数据修正有望缓和市场对通胀顽固性的担忧。与此同时，明年上半年的基数效应同样偏于有
利，瑞银预计未来六个⽉将呈现稳步去通胀态势。上述因素叠加，使得美联储采取⻓期连续加
息的必要性明显下降。
强劲 PMI 数据为企业盈利提供⽀撑
触发本轮收益率上⾏的经济数据，在瑞银看来同样具有正⾯含义。标普全球美国综合 PMI 闪值 9
⽉升⾄ 58.4 ，连续四个⽉加速扩张，为 2021 年 7 ⽉以来最强劲的私营部⻔活动扩张，印证了美国
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
经济持续稳健运⾏的判断。
瑞银认为，韧性⼗⾜的经济活动为企业收⼊和利润增⻓提供了有利环境，叠加⼈⼯智能投资持
续带来的额外推动⼒，预计标普 500 指数今年盈利增⻓ 25% ， 2027 年增⻓ 14% 。从全球视⻆看，
MSCI 全球指数今年盈利增速预测为 26% ，明年为 14% ，盈利周期的整体向好应能⽀撑全球股市
在未来 6 ⾄ 12 个⽉继续⾛⾼。
收益率上升为固定收益创造结构性机会
瑞银指出，尽管债务规模担忧是本轮债券收益率上⾏的因素之⼀，但⾼债务⽔平并不⾃动意味
着较差的投资回报，具体影响取决于各国政府的债务管理⽅式。
在此背景下，瑞银维持对固定收益资产 " 具吸引⼒ " 的评级，认为较⾼的初始收益率为投资组合提
供了稳健的收⼊基础。对于注重收益的投资者，短久期债券可有效降低利率⻛险；与此同时，

瑞银也看到中⻓久期⾼质量债券以及评级较强的投资级发⾏⼈中期信⽤债的战术性机会，建议
投资者把握当前收益率⽔平布局。
法德利差⾛阔，欧元区资产现分化
在欧洲市场，法德 10 年期国债利差周三扩⼤ 6 个基点⾄ 110 个基点，为 2012 年 7 ⽉以来最⾼⽔
平，彼时时任欧洲央⾏⾏⻓⻢⾥奥 · 德拉吉曾誓⾔ " 不惜⼀切代价 " 捍卫欧元。法国 10 年期国债收
益率上涨 15 个基点⾄ 4.66% ，涨幅明显⾼于德国国债。
瑞银认为，此次利差⾛阔主要反映市场对法国财政状况及明年总统⼤选的担忧，⽽⾮单纯受全
球债券收益率上升拖累。法国财政⾚字超过 GDP 的 5% ，其 2.7 万亿欧元政府债务的融资成本⼤
幅攀升，可能导致信⽤评级进⼀步下调。⽬前法国国债收益率甚⾄⾼于 BBB 级意⼤利国债，显
⽰市场已将其定位为利率敏感型⾼⻉塔资产。尽管如此，瑞银不认为严重财政危机迫在眉睫，
并维持对欧元区股市的正⾯看法，认为经济活动改善和盈利增⻓可抵消温和上⾏收益率带来的
估值压⼒。
⻩⾦短期承压，中⻓期配置价值依存

⻩⾦承压下跌，跌破 4300 美元 / 盎司，报 4280 美元 / 盎司左右，过去⼀个⽉累计跌幅超过 8% 。美
联储鹰派加息预期及强势美元构成短期阻⼒。
对此，瑞银表⽰，在加息预期及利率 " 更⾼更久 " 的前景下，⻩⾦短期波动性可能进⼀步加⼤，但
12 个⽉维度内仍持建设性态度。央⾏持续购⾦、储备多元化需求、政府债务规模⾼企以及地缘
政治不确定性，均构成⻩⾦的中⻓期⽀撑。瑞银将⻩⾦回调⾄ 4000 美元 / 盎司附近视为投资者增
持的机会，并预测⻩⾦价格将于 2027 年 9 ⽉前后升⾄ 5400 美元 / 盎司。
限免

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，

線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic bouts
of market pressures, we view recent
move as a normalization process, not a
crisis of confidence. Attractive real and
nominal yields reinforce our view that
investors should look beyond cash and
short-duration assets and move closer

to strategic duration target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to cconcerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to

other Government bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason for yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still above average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: September 21 - 25
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 20 Sept 2026 View in browser
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The Federal Reserve raised the federal funds rate (FFR) by 25bps on Wednesday, lifting the target range to
3.75%-4.00%. The vote was unanimous. Fed Chair Kevin Warsh cited stronger growth, insufficient progress on
inflation, and rising geopolitical risk as reasons for the move. The Fed's updated projections show inflation not
returning fully to target until 2029, while growth and employment forecasts improved. Global bond yields eased a
bit.
President Trump hosts Chinese President Xi Jinping in Washington this week, with markets watching for progress

on the export-control truce, the countries' new trade and investment boards, and the long-delayed Taiwan arms
package. Attention also turns to Fedspeak, with nine officials set to hit the tape. Goolsbee, Williams, Jefferson,
Barkin, Barr, Hammack, Paulson, Bowman, and Schmid are all on the calendar.
It's a light week for economic data. Unemployment claims (Thu) and regional business surveys from Richmond
(Tue) and Kansas City (Thu) round out the domestic calendar. Flash PMIs (Wed) will offer an early read on

September activity, both domestically and overseas. Overseas, the Swiss National Bank meets Thursday.
Here's more:
(1) FedSpeak. Fed funds futures now imply 3.1 rate increases over the next 12 months and 1.8 over the next six
(chart). The odds of an October hike stand at roughly 58%, against 42% for a hold. The odds of hikes at both the
October and December meetings are at 44%. With October hike odds still close to a coin flip, this week's remarks

by the various talking Fed heads carry real weight for a potential revision of those numbers.
Kansas City Fed President Jeffrey Schmid, speaking Friday, supported this week's hike, saying elevated inflation
reflects more than just oil prices, with a broad range of goods and services also running hot. He described the
labor market as balanced and growth as solid. He is not a voter on the FOMC this year.
The 2-year Treasury yield climbed to 4.75% by Friday's close, up from 4.67% a day earlier, and remains well

above the FFR (chart).

### Page 4

(2) Unemployment Claims. Initial jobless claims (Thu), covering the week ended September 18, follow last
week's 196,000 print, the lowest since July and a break from five straight weeks above the 200,000 mark. That
reading came in below the four-week average of 203,200 (chart). Continuing claims eased to 1,730,000 for the
week ended September 4, with the four-week average at 1,774,000 (chart).
(3) Regional Business Surveys. The Federal Reserve banks of Richmond and Kansas City release their districts’

September business surveys this week. The regional M-PMI business activity index continues to track the
national M-PMI, which eased to 54.6 in August from July's 55.6 (chart).

### Page 5

(4) Flash PMIs. According to S&P Global's survey, August's final manufacturing PMI came in at 53.9, up from a
flash reading of 53.2. Services eased to a final 56.5, down from its flash print of 56.8 (chart). September's flash
PMIs (Wed) are expected to ease slightly from those levels.
Global flash and final manufacturing readings tracked closely in August. Japan's flash reading led at 55.1, while
France's lagged at 48.0 (chart).

### Page 6

(5) Durable Goods Orders. August's new orders for durable goods (Fri) likely rose to yet another record high as
a result of the AI capital spending boom. Most of the major durable goods industries are benefiting from the AI
buildout (chart).
(6) Global Interest Rates. The Swiss National Bank (Thu) is expected to hold its policy rate at 0.00% (chart).

### Page 7

## Document 229: Expectations begin to shift against the US dollar (2026-09-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Expectations begin to shift against the US dollar (2026-09-26).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-26
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#fx #usd #us #eur #gbp
Expectations begin to shift against the US dollar
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
11:03 September 14 2026 Share
Key themes for FX markets.
Market participants remain acutely aware of the risks facing the world, particularly due to energy costs,
with the price of Brent oil having risen from around USD88 to USD108 over the past month. At the
same time, confidence in US economic growth remains resolute. Nonetheless, the US dollar has edged

lower over the past month from 99.96 to 99.1. This follows stability through late-July and early-August.

### Page 2

Over the past month, the most significant move in the bilateral exchange rates that determine the DXY
US dollar index has been in the yen cross. While August’s joint intervention by the US and Japan had
little net effect, reports of Japanese authorities requesting major domestic institutional investors adjust
their portfolios and currency management to favour the yen, paired with a strengthening in Bank of
Japan policy expectations, have since proven effective.

In August, USD/JPY’s initial decline from JPY164 to JPY155 reversed quickly back to near JPY160.
But in September, USD/JPY has broken through JPY155 and tested JPY154.
The current move certainly remains contingent on the Bank of Japan delivering on policy, the market
having priced a series of hikes through to mid-2027. But, if authorities also prove successful in
resetting capital flows related to the yen, the move to JPY154 can not only be sustained but also

extended – in time.
For now we retain a cautious view, forecasting a slow descent to JPY146 at end-2028. However, as
that level is 32% above the pair’s long-term pre-pandemic average (1990-2019), there is clearly plenty
of scope for a material upside surprise for the yen. Though, the timing and scale will largely be up to
the actions of Japan’s authorities and the long-term opportunities investors perceive in Japanese
industry and real assets.
The other major determinants of the DXY index, euro and sterling, have only ground higher over the

past month. Data has generally been positive for the Euro Area and UK; although growth is, at best,
converging to trend while the US continues to outperform.
Rate expectations are similarly priced for the Euro Area, UK and US – all arguably overstate the
degree of tightening necessary. As such, a period of relative currency stability is likely into year-end
before a softer US economy and continued uptrend in European and UK growth bear fruit in 2027.

From USD1.16 today, we see euro edging higher to USD1.17 at year end, before a steady appreciation
through 2027 to USD1.21, then USD1.22 in the first half of 2028. Sterling is expected to hold at its
current rate until this December, USD1.35, and then appreciate to USD1.39 through 2027 and to
USD1.41 by mid-2028.
From their respective starting points, euro and sterling also likely have upside potential against the US
dollar. Though it will take time to show, and only if downside risks crystallise for the US economy. The

yen outperforming would arguably also benefit euro and sterling against the US dollar, pushing the
DXY index away from its 10-year average at 98.7 and focusing participants attention on the 20-year
average of 90.8, circa 8% below today’s spot rate.
The rest of Asia is also expected to strengthen against the US dollar as developed-world inflation risks
abate and future growth opportunities are focused on.
Closely tied to global growth and development, and with no concerns over inflation or its fiscal position,

Singapore’s dollar has already appreciated back near its 5-year high versus the US dollar, currently

### Page 3

Related articles
SGD1.2680. A run towards its all-time high is certainly on the cards, albeit not our base expectation,
with USD/SGD forecast to trade at SGD1.25 in late-2028 compared to 2012’s (brief) low of SGD1.22.
China’s renminbi has appreciated at a slower rate than Singapore’s dollar to date in 2026, USD/CNY
falling from CNY6.99 to CNY6.71. But we expect there are significant gains to come, with the pair
forecast to trade down to CNY6.30 in late-2028 on our baseline view, with the risks favouring a

stronger renminbi.
For the renminbi to surprise to the upside, arguably China’s authorities will need to show initiative with
policy and remove some, or all, of the downside risks for domestic demand. Although, an easing of
geopolitical tensions which the market has confidence in would also likely provide material benefit to
China’s valuation.
Asia more broadly has experienced mixed trading this year given weak starting levels and a mix of
global and domestic risks. Still, if uncertainty recedes over the forecast horizon, and key nations such

as Singapore, China, South Korea and Japan post economic wins, the rest of the region should
experience a gradual appreciation in the months that follow.
Given the development opportunities before them, in time this could transition into a lasting structural
uptrend. This would benefit the region’s purchasing power and inflation management, albeit not at the
expense of competitiveness, with productivity gains expected to outpace currency appreciation.

This analysis was first released in Westpac Economics’ September Market Outlook.

## Document 230: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

The Fed slightly revised up its growth projections for 
2026-27 and inflation projection for 2026; however, it 
still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 September 2026

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 260: Meta CTO解释Muse：回归人性交互，下一代操纵系统的核心是“意图理解”

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta CTO解释Muse：回归人性交互，下一代操纵系统的核心是“意图理解”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-25
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Andrew Bosworth 表⽰， AI 产业正陷⼊过度关注基准测试的误区，⽽ Muse 主打⽤⼾体验与
交互质感，推动⼈机交互从 “ 直接操控 ” 迈向 “ 意图理解 ” 。隐私⽅⾯， Meta 推出端对端加密的
“ 私密 AI 处理 ” 机制；针对 AI ⻛险与 AGI ， Bosworth 持理性审慎态度，驳斥灭绝论并坚守开
源⽴场。
摘要：
Meta ⾸席技术官 Andrew Bosworth 24 ⽇在⼀档播客节⽬中，就公司旗下 AI 助⼿ Muse 及更⼴泛
的 AI 战略发表了系统性阐述。当前 AI 产业过度沉迷于基准测试分数，⽽忽视了⽤⼾体验的本质
——Muse 的差异化正在于此。
Muse ⾃内测起便已获得 Bosworth 本⼈的⾼度认可，他表⽰⾃⼰迅速将⼤量⼯作负载转移⾄这⼀
平台。产品正式发布后，外部⽤⼾的反应与内部预期⾼度吻合，令其团队深感⿎舞。 Bosworth
强调， Muse 的成功并⾮来⾃模型原始智能的最⼤化，⽽是来⾃对 " 如何让⼈类更好地使⽤⼯
具 " 这⼀命题的深⼊思考。

与此同时， Bosworth 描绘了 Meta 对下⼀代⼈机交互范式的判断：从 " 直接操控 " ⾛向 " 意图理
解 " ，英语将成为新的编程语⾔。在 AI 安全与隐私层⾯， Meta 采取了若⼲值得关注的举措：公司
延迟了 Muse 的发布时间以完善隐私架构，并推出端对端加密的 " 私密 AI 处理 " 功能 —— ⽤⼾与服
务器之间的通信经过全程加密，服务器端不留存任何记录。 Bosworth 将这⼀机制定性为⾯向最
敏感应⽤场景的信任基础设施。
Muse 的差异化：体验优先，⽽⾮跑分
Bosworth 直接点名了当前 AI ⾏业的核⼼误区。 " 我们花了过去⼏年⼤量时间讨论模型的科学，讨
论谁在某个基准测试上领先 10% 或 20% ，但对⼤多数⼈⽽⾔，这根本不重要， " 他表⽰， " 重要的
是对话的质感，以及你是否信任它。 "
Muse 的设计理念由此⽽来。 Bosworth 介绍，该团队拿到⼀个强⼒基础模型后，并未单纯最⼤化
其原始性能，⽽是着重打磨使⽤体验 —— 包括对话节奏、语⽓⻛格，乃⾄⼀个具备视觉反馈功

能的 " ⼩⻆⾊ " （ little character ）。这个⻆⾊的存在并⾮纯粹装饰：对于科技早期采⽤者圈⼦以
外的⽤⼾⽽⾔，它解决了⼀个关键的认知问题 ——"AI 现在在⼯作吗？我该怎么和它互动？ "
Bosworth 还特别提及 Muse 的⼀项独特功能：它会主动向⽤⼾提供反驳观点，在给出建议后主动
呈现 " 另⼀⾯ " 。他认为这⼀特性在其他主流模型中尚未⻅到。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

从 " 直接操控 " 到 " 意图理解 " ：界⾯范式的历史性转变
Bosworth 将 Muse 及更⼴泛的 AI 助⼿定位为⼈机交互史上的范式级跃迁。他追溯⾄施乐帕克研究
中⼼为 Alto 计算机开发的图形界⾯ —— 这⼀ " 直接操控 " 范式此后被 Mac 和 Windows 沿⽤⾄今，构
成了过去半个世纪的主流交互逻辑：点击、窗⼝焦点、⼀次⼀个应⽤。
" 如果你在 PC 上关闭窗⼝时，⿏标偏离了那个 X 按钮⼀个像素，什么都不会发⽣。⼀个婴⼉看着
屏幕都知道你想点那个 X ，但计算机不知道， "Bosworth 说， " 这就是问题所在。 "
在他看来， AI 的核⼼价值在于打破这种 " ⼈适应机器 " 的单向关系。当系统能够理解⽤⼾的意图⽽
⾮仅响应精确指令时，⼈机交互进⼊了⼀个全新阶段。他将这⼀演进类⽐为编程语⾔从机器码
到汇编、再到 Python 的持续抽象化过程： " 英语正在成为新的编程语⾔ "—— 这⼀趋势在 Meta 的
AR/VR 眼镜产品线上已有具体体现： AI 助⼿被集成于系统层，⽤⼾可以通过⾃然语⾔调度空间

界⾯，⽆需⼿动移动⾯板。
私密 AI 处理： Meta 的隐私基础设施押注
在隐私架构层⾯， Bosworth 详细介绍了 Meta 推出的 " 私密处理 " 机制。其⼯作⽅式为：⽤⼾设备
与 Meta 服务器之间的通信经过端对端加密，数据在服务器端仅在 " 可信执⾏环境 " （ Trusted
Execution Environment ）内解密处理，即便是服务器操作⼈员也⽆法访问处理内容，且处理
完成后服务器端不留存任何记录。⽤⼾的上下⽂信息保存在本地设备，由⽤⼾⾃主控制。
Bosworth 坦承，这⼀⽅案存在产品层⾯的取舍 —— 它意味着 AI ⽆法跨会话保留记忆，但他认为
对于涉及⾼度敏感内容的使⽤场景，这⼀牺牲是值得的。值得注意的是， Meta 在该架构的开发
上引⼊了 Signal 协议创始⼈ Moxie Marlinspike 作为合作⽅，并承诺发布⽩⽪书供独⽴安全研究⼈
员核验，并设⽴漏洞赏⾦计划。
谈及公众信任问题， Bosworth 援引了微软的先例：这家公司在 1990 年代末因安全问题饱受诟

病，但⼗年后已成为业界安全标杆。 " 信任是步⾏⽽来，骑⻢⽽去， " 他说， " 重建信任需要⻓期
⼀致地执⾏，持续满⾜或超越⽤⼾预期。 "
AI 灭绝论与 AGI ： Bosworth 的 " 中间⽴场 "
⾯对近期在社交媒体上⼴泛传播的 AI 导致⼈类灭绝论， Bosworth 明确表达了⾃⼰的⽴场，但措
辞审慎。 " 我不能给这些⼈赋予零概率，但我⾃⼰没有看到这种可能性， " 他说：
他同时对 AGI 的实现时间线持保守态度，认为当前⾏业普遍存在 " 我们快要解决了 " 的错觉，并指
出这种错觉在 AI 历史上反复出现 —— 他以 1970 年代的 Dartmouth 会议为例，彼时与会者相信⼗
年内将实现通⽤⼈⼯智能。 " 我认为我们距离我们现在所说的 AGI 还有相当⻓的距离， " 他表⽰。
在意识与对⻬问题上， Bosworth 的⽴场接近 Yann LeCun ：他不认为当前 AI 具备意识，并警告将
AI 拟⼈化、赋予其情感或意识属性的做法，可能从对⻬⻆度产⽣负⾯后果。他将 " 对⻬ " 简化为⼀

个务实定义：⼯具是否做了你想让它做的事。 " 如果它不对⻬，你就不会⽤它。这是⼈类历史上
每⼀件产品都经历过的强⼤约束⼒。 "
开源与 " 给⼈以⼒量 " ： Meta 的 AI 政治哲学
" 我在这个技术⾥⼯作，对它的底层机制有相当深⼊的理解，我对它感到放⼼。 "

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

——" 给予⼈们⼒量，让世界更加开放和互联 "—— 认为 " 给⼈以⼒量 " 才是这句话中更根本的锚

绕开了原始数据训练的巨⼤成本，因此在⾏业内引发了关于知识产权与资源公平的讨论。但他

显然认为开放获取的价值⾼于这⼀代价。

## Document 283: Rising risk of a Fed rate hike

potentially as early as this month if

there is no clear sign of disinflation.

multiple hikes, given our view that 
disinflation is likely to continue into next 
year as the impact of tariffs and high oil 
prices fades. 
 Thus, any such ‘defensive’ rate hike 
is likely to be neutral for equity and 
bond markets, which are already partly 
anticipating one.

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: Contained bond volatility, robust earnings

As long as US bond market volatility remains contained, the equity rally is likely to continue amid robust earnings growth  
S&P500 index, US bond market volatility (inverted)    Consensus estimates for S&P500 earnings and revenue growth

Source: Bloomberg, LSEG I/B/E/S, Standard Chartered

Editorial 
Rising risk of a Fed rate hike 
Strategy summary: Fed Chair Warsh has raised the chance of 
a rate hike this year, potentially as early as this month if August 
payrolls remain resilient and if there is no clear sign of 
disinflation. However, we see little scope for multiple hikes, 
given our view that disinflation is likely to continue into next year 
as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices fade. Thus, any such 
‘defensive’ rate hike is likely to be neutral for equity and bond 
markets which are already partly anticipating one.   
We would adopt a ‘buy on dips’ strategy for equities on any 
seasonal or pre-election volatility spike amid a robust earnings 
outlook. In bonds, we gradually extend the maturity profile to 3-
7 years as yields in this segment have risen to attractive levels.   
Setting the stage for a Fed hike: At Jackson Hole, Fed Chair 
Warsh opened the door to a potential rate hike, noting that rates 
are not restrictive, employment remains near full and underlying 
inflation is still elevated. We see just over 50% chance of a rate 
hike, potentially on 16 September, if key US data before then 
are supportive – specifically, if August payrolls are positive, 
unemployment stays near 4.1% and core inflation is at least 
0.2% m/m. If these conditions are met, not hiking could trigger 
another long- end bond selloff and undermine Warsh’s 
credibility. Meanwhile, a rate hike under these conditions is 
likely to have limited market impact, as money markets are 
already pricing over 50% chance of September hike. 
Alternative scenario: Conversely, another month of negative 
payrolls in August or core inflation cooling below 0.2% m/m 
would allow neutral -to-dovish Fed members to push back 
against a rate hike this month. In that case, December would 
be the next stop for reassessment ; a rate hike in October, 
weeks before the November mid-term elections, is unlikely. 
Little scope for multiple hikes:  Even if the Fed delivers a 
‘defensive’ hike this year, we see little scope for further 
increases as US inflation should ease towards the 2% target 
next year when tariff and oil-price effects fade. New York Fed 
President Williams and Governor Waller’s refusal to pre-commit 
to a September or a future hike underscores the disinflationary 
outlook. Also, multiple rate rises could prove counterproductive 
by lifting already record US government borrowing costs as the 
Treasury continues to shorten maturities of its bond issues. 
Gradually extend maturity in bonds: The latest rise in short-
to-medium term bond yields is thus an opportunity to gradually 
extend maturities in bonds, given our outlook for disinflation and 
limited upside for policy rates. We extend our preferred maturity 
bucket to 3-7 years from 3- 5 years. We continue to prefer 
corporate bonds, where we are comfortable in going down to 
BB-rated USD-denominated credit, which offer 6.5-7.5% yield. 
Buying any September dip in equities. Rising bond yields , 
seasonal weakness in September and pre- midterm election 
blues are potential near-term headwinds for equities. However, 
equities have historically held up well despite higher bond yields 
as long as bond volatility is contained and growth resilient.  
In fact, history suggests taking advantage of any weakness in 
September to build long-term exposure as underlying earnings 
growth prospects remain solid (20%-plus S&P500 EPS growth 
expected in Q3 and Q4). For instance, over the past 15 years, 
buying the S&P 500 index at its September low has generated 
an average return of 11.4% over the subsequent six months. If 
we focus only on years when September ended in negative 
territory, the average six-month return rises to 15.1%.  
ECB to hike again next week. While a Fed rate hike remains 
a toss-up, the ECB is highly likely to hike 25bps to 2.5% next 
week. Euro area Q2 GDP expanded an above-trend 0.4% q/q, 
as the region weathered the energy shock better than feared. 
The momentum has carried into Q3. August headline inflation 
climbed to a near-three-year-high of 3.3% y/y on energy prices, 
although core inflation eased to 2.4% as second-round effects 
remain absent. With no progress seen in resolving the Middle 
East conflict, the case for ECB taking further pre-emptive action 
has risen. We remain focused on whether President Lagarde 
pushes back against market expectations of further rate hikes.    
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 4 September 2026

Top client questions 
  F ollowing Fed Chair Warsh’s debut keynote address at  
Jackson Hole, do you expect a Fed rate hike this month? 
Our view: We  have raised our probability of a September Fed 
rate hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech, as 
we believe the bar for keeping rates unchanged is now high. 
Rationale: The Fed’s price-stability mandate will be put to the test  
at its  16 September meeting. Fed Chair Warsh is expected to 
demonstrate his  inflation-fighting resolve, consistent with his 
Jackson Hole remarks that summer’s encouraging inflation readings 
were insufficient to confirm  a structural disinflationary trend, 
reaffirming the central bank’s focus on delivering price stability.  
Warsh’s comments lead us to believe that the bar for leaving rates 
unchanged is now high. A hold would likely require a major labour 
market deterioration and a materially softer inflation print in incoming 
data releases; in-line data would not be interpreted as dovish. 
However, in our view, a  rate hike would not mark the start of a 
sustained tightening cycle, as we expect disinflationary pressures 
and labour market weakness to persist. This view is also reflected 
in the US bond yield curve’s reaction to Warsh’s remarks. The curve 
bear-flattened, with short -term yields rising more than long-term 
yields. As yields in the sub- 10-year segment have moved higher 
relative to those in other parts of the curve, we suggest corporate 
bond investors to take advantage of the move by extending duration 
from the 3-5- year bucket to the 3-7-year bucket. We believe this 
segment is short enough to avoid the volatility and crowded 
positioning at the long end, yet long enough to benefit if the Fed 
remains on hold, given the short end of the curve is pricing in a hike. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Would the US 30-year government bond yield break above 
its 20-year high of 5.4%? 
Our view: We believe the US 30-year government bond yield is 
unlikely to remain  above 5.4% sustainably in the short term. 
However, we expect US long -end bond yields to stay elevated 
and the US 30-year bond yield to range between  5.25% and 
5.50% over the next 12 months. 
Rationale: In the near term, with the US Treasury’s long-end bond 
buyback programme in place, we expect temporary moves above 
5.4%, but these are unlikely to be sustained.  
In the longer term, the effectiveness of the buyback programme is 
questionable given structural headwinds – a deteriorating US fiscal 
trajectory, waning foreign demand, AI -driven corporate supply and 
sticky inflation amplified by the Middle East conflict – which argue for 
yields remaining elevated even if the buyback program me limits 
the most acute spikes. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Long-end yields rose less than short-end post Fed 
Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole comments

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 4 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Taiwan is one of the few tech -heavy equity markets that 
recovered the ground lost in July. Is it time to take profit or add? 
Our view: We remain constructive on Taiwan and continue to 
view it as an attractive Opportunistic idea.  
Rationale: The MSCI Taiwan Index rebounded by 4.5% in August  
after declining 3.9% in July , in TWD terms. Recent guidance from 
hyperscalers indicate s that AI infrastructure spending remains 
resilient, with few signs of a slowdown. Taiwan remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI infrastructure buildout through its leadership 
in semiconductor manufacturing and the AI hardware ecosystem. 
Beyond foundries, many Taiwan companies are critical suppliers  
of printed circuit boards, liquid cooling and networking solutions, all 
of which benefit from rising AI hardware complexity and deployment.  
Although investors have raised concerns around circular financing, 
we view this largely as a reflection of AI's capital-intensive nature, 
in which incumbent technology firms provide funding and strategic 
support to ecosystem partners to accelerate product development  
and capacity expansion. While MSCI Taiwan’s price- to-earnings 
(P/E) ratio remains above its five-year average, valuations have 
moderated from June’s peak following the recent correction. This is 
supported by a stronger growth outlook  (2025-28E compound 
annual growth rate of 40.4% vs. 5.0% in 2021-25). As such , we 
remain constructive on Taiwan and its pivotal role in the global AI 
manufacturing ecosystem.   
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
 Amid rising commodity prices, do you see  any 
opportunities within energy and commodity equities? 
Our view: We favour maintaining energy and commodity sector 
allocations closer to long term strategic benchmark weights  
rather than taking a significant Overweight position.  
Rationale: While there may be selective opportunities within the 
energy sector amid rising commodity prices, we favour maintaining 
allocations closer to strategic benchmark weights rather than 
taking a significant Overweight position. The binary nature of the 
near-term oil price outlook and any changes in geopolitical relations 
can create significant volatility. We also expect oil prices to ease 
towards USD 70/bbl over the next 12 months as transit conditions 
normalise, alongside a sizeable supply surplus expected in 2027. 
While i nvestors should not ignore energy stocks from a portfolio 
construction perspective , we continue to favo ur sectors where 
earnings visibility is stronger , such as t echnology and 
financials. We also take a more selective approach within materials, 
with gold reinstated to Overweight as the price outlook has notably 
improved alongside a sharp pullback in the USD. We have raised 
our three- and 12-month gold price targets to USD 4,750/oz  and 
USD 5,000/oz, respectively.  
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 4 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Which currencies stand to benefit the most  as more 
central banks adopt a hawkish stance? 
Our view: The AUD appears best placed to benefit from further 
hawkish repricing, as additional RBA tightening remains only 
partly priced in. JPY upside risks have increased, but markets 
have already discounted substantial BoJ tightening, limiting the 
scope for a fresh policy surprise. The EUR retains some policy 
support, while NZD upside looks more limited following the 
RBNZ’s less hawkish forward guidance. 
Rationale: Currencies typically benefit most when central banks 
tighten more than markets expect. For Australia, Bloomberg pricing 
implies only around 12.7bps of additional RBA tightening, leaving 
greater scope for AUD upside if inflation or activity data prompt a 
more hawkish RBA path. 
For the JPY, recent hawkish repricing and official resistance to 
excessive weakness are supportive.  However, with substantial 
BoJ tightening already priced in, further sustained gains would  
require a faster or larger tightening path than currently discounted. 
The EUR would similarly need stronger guidance on further 
tightening to extend gains, while NZD upside looks more limited after 
the RBNZ’s projected rate path proved shallower than expected. 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
 How does the outcome of the recent G20 Finance Ministers 
and Central Bank Governors meeting affect your oil outlook? 
Our View: The lack of a diplomatic breakthrough at the  recent 
G20 meeting suggests geopolitical risk premi a will persist. As 
geopolitics remains the dominant driver of crude oil prices, we 
expect West Texas Intermediate (WTI)  oil to trade toward s the 
upper end of our USD 70-90/bbl range in the near term. 
Rationale: The  recent G20 finance track produced no movement 
towards conflict de-escalation in t he Middle East . Instead, the 
emphasis shifted towards tighter financial and economic 
pressure on Iran. While this reduces the risk of an extended military 
campaign, it points to a more prolonged period of supply 
uncertainty.  
Market estimates put total Gulf flows near 9.5 mb/d – around 50% 
of the pre- conflict norm  – supported by alternative routing and 
reported ‘dark transit s’, which are helping offset some supply 
disruptions. However, declining inventory buffers should keep WTI 
supported around USD 90/bbl. Given China is a key buyer of Iranian 
crude, the Trump-Xi summit later this month is the next major event 
to watch, as any shift in China's stance towards US pressure on Iran 
could materially affect export flows and oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

## Document 327: 「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」要請から3年の現在地（後編） ～投資家の評価と会社の行動からみる次の課題～

Source PDF: all report pdf/「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」要請から3年の現在地（後編） ～投資家の評価と会社の行動からみる次の課題～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 9 月 17 日

1/7 
「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」要請から 3 年の現在地（後編） 
～投資家の評価と会社の行動からみる次の課題～ 
 政策調査部 シニア研究員  河谷 善夫

（要旨） 
○ 前編では、東証の「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」要請後のPBR・ROE の変化を確認し、
改善が会社全体に一様に広がったわけではない一方、投資家から取組みの進展を評価された
会社では、とりわけ PBR 改善との対応が比較的明瞭であることを示した。後編では、投資家
が会社の何を評価しているのか、会社が投資家との対話をどのように経営行動に結びつけて
いるのかを分析する。 
○ まず、投資家から取組みの進展を評価された 295 社へのコメントを「資本配分」「事業ポー
トフォリオ改革」 「資本効率経営」 「経営陣のリーダーシップ」 「開示・対話の内容、姿勢」
の 5 カテゴリーに分類し、各カテゴリーの該当・非該当会社で PBR・ROE 改善率を比較した。 
○ その結果、事業ポートフォリオ改革やリーダーシップではPBR 改善率の差が比較的大きい一
方、資本配分や資本効率経営ではROE 改善率の差が大きいなど、評価された取組みによって
改善の現れ方に違いがみられた。開示・対話の内容、姿勢では単独での改善率差は小さく、
個別施策の効果を単純に切り分けることは難しい。 
○ 一方、投資家の評価は複数の取組みにまたがるケースが多い。例えば、事業ポートフォリオ
改革を評価された会社の63.2％は資本効率経営でも評価され、 資本配分を評価された会社の
66.0％も資本効率経営で評価されていた。また、単一カテゴリーのみの会社に比べ、複数カ
テゴリーで評価された会社では PBR・ROE 改善率が高い傾向もみられた。 
○ さらに会社側 682 社へのアンケートをみると、会社が認識する投資家の関心と実際の取組み
との関係は、PBR・ROE によるグループによって異なる。全体では、事業戦略・成長戦略や成
長分野への投資について、投資家の関心率が会社の実施率を大きく上回る一方、株主・投資
家との対話では会社の実施率が関心率を上回っていた。 
○ これらから、資本コストを意識し、開示や対話を行うこと自体にとどまらず、そこで得られ
た示唆を事業ポートフォリオ、資本配分、成長投資など具体的な経営判断に落とし込み、持
続的な収益性と市場評価の向上につなげることが次の課題と考えられる。「対話すること」
から「経営に実装すること」へ、取組みの実効性がより問われている。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
今回、河谷（2026）の分析枠組みを踏襲しつつ、対象をプライム市場からスタンダード市場ま
で広げた2,903 社について、 「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」の現在地を前後編の2 回にわた
って考察している（注1）。前編では、東証要請後の改善が市場全体に一様に広がったわけではな
い一方、投資家から取組みの進展を評価された会社では、とりわけPBR改善との対応が比較的明瞭
であることを示した。ただし、投資家からの評価とPBR改善との因果関係を示すものではない。 
後編となる本稿では、2026 年8 月に東証が公表した、投資家が取組みの進展を評価した会社リ
ストとその評価内容、ならびに会社向けアンケート調査結果を用いて分析を深める。投資家が何
を評価しているのか、会社が投資家との対話をどのように経営行動に結びつけているのかを考察
し、取組みの現在地と今後の課題を確認する。

2. 投資家は何を評価しているのか 
本章の分析では、投資家から取組みの進展を評価された各会社（295 社、注2）に関するコメン
トについて、生成AIを補助的に用いて内容を整理し、投資家の評価観点を「資本配分」 「事業ポー
トフォリオ改革」 「資本効率経営」 「経営陣のリーダーシップ」 「開示・対話の内容、姿勢」の5 つ
のカテゴリーに分類した（以下、本文及び図表では「開示・対話」と略記する。注3）。各カテゴ
リーに該当する会社と該当しない会社について、2023 年2 月末から2026 年 3 月末までのPBR・ROE
改善率を比較したものが資料1 である（注4）。

資料 1 各カテゴリーに該当する会社と該当しない会社のPBRとROEの改善率の比較 
<PBR>

<ROE>

（出所）東証公表資料及びBloombergデータより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 3

3/7 
この比較で注目されるのは、投資家が評価した取組みによって、PBRとROEの改善の現れ方が異
なる点である。事業ポートフォリオ改革や経営陣のリーダーシップではPBR改善率の差が相対的に
大きい一方、資本配分や資本効率経営ではROE改善率の差が大きい。これに対し、開示・対話では
単独での改善率差は小さい。こうした結果からは、投資家が評価する取組みによって、収益性の
改善と市場からの再評価との対応の仕方が異なる傾向がうかがえる。 
もっとも、各会社の評価コメントには複数の取組みが同時に含まれており、この比較から個々
の施策の独立した効果を識別することはできない。そこで次章では、個別の取組みを切り分ける
のではなく、複数の取組みがどのように併せて評価されているかを確認する。

3. 投資家評価にみられる取組みの複合性 
資料 2 左は、各カテゴリーが他のカテゴリーとどの程度重複して評価されているかを示す共起
表である。

資料 2 5 つのカテゴリーの共起表と投資家評価カテゴリー数別PBR・ROEの改善率の比較 
<共起表>     <投資家評価カテゴリー数別PBR・ROEの改善率の比較>

（出所）資料 1 と同じ

例えば、事業ポートフォリオ改革について評価された会社68 社のうち、63.2％にあたる43 社
は資本効率経営についても評価されている。また、資本配分について評価されている188 社のう
ち、66.0％にあたる124 社は資本効率経営でも評価されているということがわかる。リーダーシ
ップが評価されている会社の60.4％が開示・対話でも評価されている。 
次に、単一カテゴリーのみで評価された会社に比べ、複数カテゴリーで評価された会社では、
PBR・ROEの改善率が高い傾向がみられる。また、獲得票数が1 票の会社より、2 票以上の会社の方
が改善率は高い（資料3）。

### Page 4

4/7 
資料 3 評価された投資家数(票数)別PBR・ROEの改善率の比較

（出所）資料 1 と同じ

これらの結果は、投資家が会社の取組みを単一の施策ではなく、複数の経営課題への対応の組
み合わせとして評価していることを示唆している。また、単一カテゴリーのみで評価された会社
より、複数カテゴリーで評価された会社の方がPBR・ROEの改善率は高い傾向にある。 
ただし、この結果を「評価される取組みの数が多いほどPBR・ROEが改善する」と直ちに解釈す
ることには注意が必要である。複数の投資家から評価された会社では、評価コメントに含まれる
論点も増え、結果として該当カテゴリー数が多くなる可能性があるためである。今回のデータだ
けでは、取組みの多様さと投資家からの評価の強さ（票数）の影響を切り分けることはできな
い。したがって、ここでは、複数の取組みを併せて評価された会社では、PBR・ROEの改善率が相
対的に高い傾向がみられると捉えるのが適切であろう。

4.  対話を企業価値向上につなげるための課題 
ここまでは投資家側から評価された会社の特徴をみてきた。次に視点を会社側に移し、東証が
実施した会社向けアンケートから、会社が認識する投資家の関心と実際の取組みとの関係を確認
する。アンケート回答会社は 682 社であり、個社名は示されていないものの、それぞれが属する
PBR・ROEのグループは示されている。第1 グループ 294 社、第2 グループ106 社、第3 グループ
215 社、第4 グループ67 社となっている。 
このアンケートでは、 「投資家の自社の取組みに対する関心事項」と「対話で得られた意見を踏
まえて実施した取組み」に共通する14 項目がある（注5） 。本稿では、そのうち、事業戦略、収益
性、成長投資、資本政策、対話という主要な経営課題をカバーする5 項目を取り上げる。 
資料 4 は、投資家がその事項に対して関心を持っていると認識した会社のうち、実際に同じ事
項について取組みを実施した会社の割合（対応率）である。

### Page 5

5/7 
資料 4 5 つの事項についての対応率の状況

（出所）東証公表データより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

例えば、低PBR・低ROE会社では「既存事業の収益性向上」の対応率が相対的に高い一方、高
PBR・高ROE会社では「成長分野への投資」の対応率が比較的高い。すべての項目で第1 グルー
プ会社の対応率が高いわけではなく、会社が置かれた状況によって投資家との対話を踏まえて
重点を置く取組みも異なっていることがうかがえる。 
次に、同じ5 つの事項について、投資家がその事項に関心を持った割合と、会社が実施した
割合の差（関心率－実施率）を示したのが資料5 である。

資料 5 5 つの事項についての投資家の関心率と会社の実施率の差

（出所）資料 4 と同じ

資料5 からは、全体では、 「事業戦略・成長戦略」で関心率が実施率を44.0％Pt、 「成長分野
への投資」でも35.5％Pt上回る。一方、 「株主・投資家との対話」は-19.2％Ptで、会社の実施
率が関心率を上回っている。これは、株主・投資家との対話自体には会社側が幅広く取り組ん
でいる一方、会社が認識する投資家の関心を踏まえ、事業戦略や成長投資など具体的な経営行
動につなげる余地はなお大きいことを示唆している。

5.  最後に～対話から実装へ 
本シリーズでは、東証が公表したデータを用いて、 「資本コストや株価を意識した経営」の現

### Page 6

6/7 
在地を確認してきた。前編では、東証要請後のPBR・ROEの変化を確認し、改善が会社全体に一
様に広がったわけではない一方、投資家から取組みの進展を評価された会社では、PBR・ROEの
改善も相対的に進んでいることを確認した。特にプライム市場上場会社に限定して比較して
も、PBR1 倍割れからの脱却との対応は比較的明瞭であった。 
後編ではさらに、投資家が評価する内容と会社側の対応をみた。投資家の評価は、資本配
分、事業ポートフォリオ改革、資本効率経営、経営陣のリーダーシップ、開示・対話など複数
の取組みにまたがっており、PBRとROEの改善の現れ方にも違いがみられた。 
一方、会社側アンケートからは、投資家との対話を踏まえた取組みがみられる一方、事業戦
略・成長戦略や成長分野への投資などでは、投資家の関心と会社の実施との間になおギャップ
があることも確認された。 
これらを踏まえると、東証の要請後の次の課題は、資本コストを意識して開示や投資家との
対話を行うことに加え、そこで得られた示唆を具体的な経営判断へ落とし込むことにあると考
えられる。事業ポートフォリオの見直し、資本配分、成長投資などを自社の経営課題に応じて
実行し、それらを持続的な収益性と市場評価の向上につなげられるか。 「対話すること」から
「経営に実装すること」へ、取組みの実効性がこれまで以上に問われるようになっているとい
えよう。 
以 上

【注釈】 
1) 本稿の分析では、前編と同様、2023 年2 月末および2026 年 7 月末の双方で東証プライム市場
またはスタンダード市場に属する会社のうち、分析に必要なPBR・ROEデータを取得できた
2,903 社を対象としている。 
2) 前編で示したとおり、投資家により取組みの進展を評価された会社は300 社であるが、今回の
分析対象会社とした2,903 社に含まれるのは295 社である。 
3) 投資家が各会社について評価したコメントを生成AIに読み込ませ、コメントに共通してみられ
る論点や用語を基に評価観点を整理した。その上で、生成AIによる整理を参考に筆者が「資本
配分」 「事業ポートフォリオ改革」 「資本効率経営」 「経営陣のリーダーシップ」 「開示・対話の
内容、姿勢」の5 カテゴリーを設定し、各コメントについて該当するカテゴリーを分類した。
なお、一つのコメントが複数の論点を含む場合には複数カテゴリーへの該当を認め、生成AIに
よる判定が困難または曖昧なものについては、筆者が原文を確認して最終的に判断した。 
4) なお、各カテゴリーの該当・非該当間の改善率について二群の比率差検定を行ったところ、5％
水準で有意な差は確認されなかった。このため、以下では改善率の違いを傾向として捉えてい
る。 
5) この 2 つの質問に含まれる回答事項は「投資家の自社の取組みに対する関心事項」が15 個、 「対
話で得られた意見を踏まえて実施した取組み」が14 個である。前者の中の「短期的業績見通し」だ
けが後者の回答事項に含まれていない。その他はすべて対応している。

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

主題報告概述 : 本篇報告將分析 2026年美元現況 ，最終形成
2027年美元看法。以下為本篇報告內容概要:
(1) 匯市現況：美國伊朗戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，美元走強。
(2) 政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對美元的影響。
(3) 期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰爭。
(4) 美元基本面：景氣與通膨循環將維持在金髮女孩經濟環境。
(5) 美元技術面：技術面跌破年線，籌碼面偏多但8月轉弱。
(6) 非美匯市: 歐洲政治面利空及日本阻貶為美元提供底部支撐
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、

油價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治
與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策、
美伊衝突及烏俄戰爭等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左
右美元的重要變數 。2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，
而是在高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境
穩健，景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升
級事件，美元指數預期維持97–105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓
力、下有支撐 」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當
通膨回落與利率下降時美元偏弱。

(1)匯市現況 : 美伊戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，
美元走強
匯市現況: 2026年美元走勢主要受到美國經濟基本面、Fed貨幣
政策預期、川普政策以及地緣政治風險交互影響。
2026年初，川普重返貿易壁壘政策 、美國就業動能放緩且通膨
趨穩，市場普遍預期降息循環延續 ，使美元維持偏弱整理格局 ，
美元指數於1月27日一度跌至全年低點95.55。期間宣布華許出
任Fed主席的消息曾短暫帶動美元反彈 ，但整體仍未擺脫區間
震盪格局 。然而，2月28日伊朗事件爆發 ，3月9日西德州原油
一度接近每桶 120美元，能源價格上升帶動全球通膨預期升溫 ，
美國降息預期下降。進入3-4月，美國經濟數據進一步展現韌性，
製造業 、消費及投資表現改善 ，Fed季度預測亦同步上修 GDP

成長與通膨展望 。受此影響 ，利率期貨隱含年底前降息預期由
原先降息2碼逐步修正至不降息 ，推動美元於2Q26展開反彈。
同時 ，受油價飆升影響 ，9月Fed季度預測將今年 PCE上修至
3.7%，官員點陣圖亦上修利率路徑 ，市場開始討論通膨壓力可
能迫使Fed重新升息 。匯市焦點亦逐漸轉向新任聯準會主席華
許提出的新貨幣政策框架 。在Fed政策預期持續調整下 ，美元
指數於6月24日升至全年高點101.8，隨後進入高檔盤整格局。

(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(2/3)
數席次機率下行趨勢可見 ，當川普政策面無法政治上快速解決
問題並且影響民生時，取得參眾議院的機率加速下行。
期中選舉結果對美元影響 ：觀察1970年以來美國期中選舉結束
後一年美元之平均表現。
民主黨主導兩院 ：當民主黨期中選舉後在參眾議院均為多數 ，
美元指數平均下跌 -4.6%，6次有4次為下跌 ，且前一年美元均
下跌。1971、1987及2007年為下跌幅度最大的3年。
1971年尼克森(共和黨)總統宣布停止黃金兌換 ，10%進口附加
關稅，迫使盟友重啟貿易與貨幣談判(最終達成史密松寧協定)，
美元下跌-7.8%。

1987年雷根(共和黨)延續1985年廣場協議後美元貶值趨勢延續 。
雙赤字壓力 、通膨升溫 、利率上升以及外資拋售壓力導致發生
1987年10月19日黑色星期一股市崩盤 ，美元指數當年下跌 -
17.6%(1986年下跌-16.1%)。
2007年布希(共和黨)總統時期面臨雙赤字 、非美利差縮小 ，以
及次貸風暴風險升溫。美元下跌-8.3%(2006年下跌-8.2%)。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(3/3)
共和黨、民主黨各主導一議院 ：當美國國會進入兩黨各主導一
議院時期，美元指數期中選舉過後一年平均上漲 3.6%，4次有3
次為上漲，且前一年4次美元均為上漲。美國期中選舉若造成國
會由不同政黨掌控 ，通常會限制總統隨意推動大規模擴張性財
政政策與赤字支出 。這類財政節制有助於降低通膨失控風險 ，
進而支撐美元走強。4次國會分裂時段其中較大美元升值趨勢為
1983年雷根(共和黨)時期。 1980年以來聯準會主席保羅伏克爾
採取強力升息手段打擊通膨 ，美國利率保持在相對高檔一段時
間且通膨已逐步受到控制 (從1980年15%降至4%以下)。美元

1983年升值14.9%(1982年升值12.1%)。1983年後，美國經濟
順利擺脫先前的嚴重衰退 ，展開強勁增長 ，市場對美國資產信
心大增，形成所謂強勢美元格局 。美元指數在80年代高點轉折
時期美國實質利率(基準利率-消費者物價指數)已從高檔下降。
小結：2026年期中選舉結果可能形成國會分裂 ，國會分裂長期
有助抑制赤字與通膨 ，因此長期對美元有利 ，但短期可能因預
算協商及債務上限僵局增加政治風險 ，對美元造成波動。2027
年將再度面臨美國債務上限 ，以及美伊地緣政治 、貿易戰的變
數均將牽動實質利率的變化 ，因此接下來將進一步分析選後影
響美元的關鍵因子。
✓

對美元有利
✓

隨美元趨勢走弱
✓

涉及特定投資標的之建議。
Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(1/3)
期中選舉過後影響美元的關鍵因子聚焦 財政面 (政府關門事件)、
貿易戰(法院裁定關稅無效後宣布以其他方式課徵關稅)、地緣政
治面(伊朗戰爭觸發油價上揚)。
財政面：8月國會通過持續撥款決議至 12月11日，代表政府關
門的風險將落在11月3日後，但新國會於2027年1月3日任職，
因此很可能選後再延後撥款決議 。除此之外 ，根據兩黨政策中
心預測，2027年9月後可能再度面臨美國債務上限議題 ，屆時
面臨2028年預算審核，參眾議院的分治恐延長達成共識的時間
以及提升美國主權債信評或展望調降風險 。從全國納稅人聯盟

(NTU)情境分析預測，美國聯邦政府收支很可能在2027年2月至
7月觸及債務上限。一旦債務達到法定限額，財政部長將宣布暫
停發行債務期 。在暫停發行債務期間內 ，財政部不得增加受法
定限額約束的債務 ，但到期債務仍可展期 。計算美國財政部透
過動用收入 、現有現金儲備和採取非常規措施 ，真正無法履行
聯邦義務約在2028年2月。然而債務上限和債務違約日(X-Date)
何時達到存在很大的不確定性 。受債務上限約束的債務將於
2027年3月接近法定上限 500億美元以內，之後隨著4月稅收的
到來而暫時下降 ，但若政府財政赤字增加超過原先預測 ，X-
Date將會提前到來。

涉及特定投資標的之建議。
Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(2/3)
美國信評方面 ，惠譽8月將美國信評維持在 AA+評級且展望穩
定，但警告信評下調風險因子如下 ：政府債務佔GDP水準或償
債能力惡化 ，例如未能解決公共支出和收入的平衡 ，或遭受宏
觀金融衝擊 。經濟政策制定可信度下降導致削弱美元儲備貨幣
地位，從而降低政府融資彈性 。惠譽預期 ，美國債務占GDP比
重到2028年可能升至123%，較AA評級國家46.3%的債務比率
中值高出一倍以上 ，因此，美國信評或展望下修的風險有可能
同時伴隨財政惡化或經濟成長放緩 /衰退造成償債能力惡化 。
2027年美國財政議題將繼續面臨逆風 ，共和黨失去完全執政的

環境對財政有利但政治僵局則凸顯債務上限及違約的風險 ，因
此增加美元短期波動風險。
貿易戰：2026年川普貿易戰從對等關稅全面性課徵轉向法律攻
防，預期2027年貿易政策以各國與美國雙邊貿易協定談判為主
軸，以及針對進口品項實施不同的關稅 (川普8月宣布2027年起
對一系列加拿大汽車產品加徵最高達 50%的關稅)。川普貿易政
策不確定性因素延續 ，但本行認為時序進入 2027年貿易戰全面
性惡化的可能性已降低 ，且企業及投資人已對貿易政策不確定
性有一定程度的認知 ，因此傳導至資產價格的影響性亦將有所
降低。
地緣政治 ：地緣政治風險的擴散效應高於上述財政及關稅 。主
因荷姆茲海峽能源輸出量佔全球 20%，油價上漲對通膨的影響
為2027年美元走勢的主要變數 。2月底事件爆發以來原油遠期
曲線現貨溢價 ，代表短期內市場需求強烈或發生供給中斷 。目
前美伊戰爭從熱戰轉向封鎖荷姆茲海峽 。UAE及部分中東國家
已開始運用地面輸油管線避開該管線 ，但伊朗無人機打擊範圍
遠至含蓋紅海 ，倘若事件面惡化 ，紅海運輸除了葉門胡塞武裝

組織干擾，亦有伊朗無人機攻擊風險。目前美伊談判仍未達成
✓

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(3/3)
協議，伊朗拒絕交出高濃縮鈾 ，因此仍無法排除美國運用地面
部隊進行搜索 ，也無法排除伊朗透過遠端無人機攻擊美國在其
他中東國家的軍事或煉油設施 ，美國運用地面部隊恐將地緣政
治風險升級，屆時油價、利率以及通膨預期可能同步向上。
風險變數對2027年美元的影響 ：美國政府債務問題並非全新風
險因子。自1960年以來，美國國會已經累計調整法定債務上限
超過78次。然而，2027年若期中選後國會分裂 ，疊加年底預算
勢必面臨再延後撥款決議 ，屆時政府關門或違約的不確定性因
素將影響經濟成長並且降低聯準會緊縮的可能 。而美國債務上

限再度上調則可能引起信評展望調降的風險 。以美元的角度而
言，有紀律的財政計畫的確可將加強投資人對美元資產的信心 ，
對美元有利 。但短線若因此造成政府關門以及無法在預算達成
協議則對美元短線不利 ，因此定調2027年財政風險因子在分裂
國會環境，對美元而言屬於短空長多。
除了債務風險外 ，貿易戰及美伊戰爭事件的發展最終將反應在
油價及通膨的走勢。從今年油價一度飆升至每桶120美元可見，
短期油價的上漲並未造成短線通膨危機。但不可否認，高油價

(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(3/3)
對消費動能已帶來壓力 ，且美伊戰爭的不確定性因素更進一步
影響消費者信心。對美元而言，2月28日美伊戰爭以來美元指數
區間於97.6-101.8，幅度4.3%。美元與油價的相關性處於正向 ，
因此戰爭變數對美元影響頗深 。就目前趨勢而言 ，若地緣政治
能排除地面軍事行動的變數 ，美元將回歸反應經濟基本面對利
率的影響。市場對於聯準會年底利率政策的看法從 1Q26戰爭前
預期降息至今預期升息近3碼，消費者物價指數年增率則上升約
1%至3.36%(聯準會對於PCE今明兩年的預測為3.7%、2.3%)。

若債務上限 、地緣政治 、貿易壁壘等議題未惡化 ，預期美國經
濟偏向溫和成長 ，通膨可望放緩 。觀察中國信託美國景氣動向
指數可見，3個月以來，股市動能略顯放緩，但多數領先指標仍
持續改善 。FactSet 預估2027年標普500企業獲利成長 15%，
且營收成長高達9%，企業獲利面維持高度擴張。樂觀的企業前
景繼續帶動資本支出 ，彭博社統計2027年標普500企業資本支
出年增率仍高達 20%，正向的企業獲利持續傳導到實際經濟 。
2024年以來，實際經濟數據持續改善 ，且今年已回到 0軸以上
擴張區間 。伴隨調查及價格指標持續向上 ，整體景氣動能趨勢

正向。 展望2027年，溫和成長伴隨通膨放緩美元處於盤整偏弱
格局，基本情境內貨幣政策則傾向維持基準利率穩定。

2026/9
義大利民粹政黨執政時期10
年公債殖利率與歐元區利差
30%，歐洲政治生態將進入高度不穩定與兩極化的狀態 。在歐
洲多黨內閣制或雙首長制國家中 ，30%是傳統主流政黨能否順
利執政的關鍵臨界點 。進入2027年，歐盟將有 7國舉行國會選

舉，1月30日德國總統選舉以及4月18日法國總統大選。民粹主
義右翼政黨正在崛起 ，並可能在多個國家大幅增加席次 。每次
選舉結果都將影響各國政治局勢 ，進而反映整個歐洲的政治環
境。然而，右翼民粹主義的崛起對金融市場也並非絕對利空 ，
義大利右翼政黨取得政權後堅持財政紀律 ，向金融市場展現負
責任的治理 ，同時關注移民和文化衝突等社會議題 ，以維繫基
層支持者 。義大利和歐元區 10年期公債利差從義大利右翼政黨
勝選時2.45%至今已收斂至0.83%。此外，許多右翼民粹主義政
黨也緩和了其疑歐立場 ，不再主張退出歐元區或歐盟 。2027年
法國總統大選為首要政治議題。法國極右翼領袖勒龐(Le Pen)與
她所屬的政黨國民聯盟 (RN)近年來調整了政綱 ，已放棄脫歐主
張，並且在財政上淡化激進政策 ，強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規
範，將財政赤字控制在GDP 3%以下。整體而言，歐洲政治光譜
進入兩極化 ，法國選後議會仍需籌組多數聯盟 ，財政惡化風險
可控，但若極左派梅蘭雄意外勝選，大幅增加政府支出、對富

債作為抵押 ， 讓 日 本 在 不 出 售 美 國 公 債 的 情 況 下 買 入 日 圓
(FIMA-外國與國際貨幣當局附買回機制 )。目前日本持有 1.1兆
美元公債 ，同時加上美國政府表態支持干預弱勢日圓 ，美日聯
手讓日圓短線貶勢暫時告一段落 。美元兌日圓9月在160再度形

成一波回檔，顯示美日政府力守160整點關卡的決心。目前日本
利率(無擔保隔夜拆款利率)1.25%水準再同時加入日圓隔夜指數交
換利率隱含升息3碼，2027年利率水準與歐美差距仍有一段距離，
實質利率仍為負值，且在成熟市場中偏低。除此之外，高市政府
擴張性財政政策及新增支出計畫恐持續不利日圓 。高市於7月30
日宣布自2027年4月起，將現行8%食品消費稅率降至1%，實施
期限為2年。兩年加總稅收將減少10兆日圓，以2025年總稅收而
言，每年約擴大赤字 -6%。除此之外 ，國防預算計畫每年以
2%GDP編列，預估2027年將達14兆日圓。同時，國家級長期戰
略投資計畫於2040年前投入370兆日圓支持17大戰略領域，整體
財政方針不利日圓中長期表現。

重點提示
(6)非美匯市 : 歐洲政治不確定性及日本阻貶為
美元提供底部支撐(5/5)
✓
日圓長期轉為多頭
關鍵因子則將落在
日本政府是否開始
運用政策引導海外
投資資金回流
可視為第一波技術面日圓多方關卡。就基本面而言，政府干預匯
市無法改變貨幣政策與財政政策的路徑。若日本通膨無法帶動日
本央行加速升息，日圓利率水準則無法形成資金流入的誘因。以
日圓長期轉為多頭關鍵因子則將落在日本政府是否開始運用政策
引導海外投資資金回流(日本財務省統計到2025年底，日本在海
外的淨資產達近562兆日圓)，將是長期影響日圓走勢的最有效政
策。
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油

價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與
高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策
及美伊衝突等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左右美元的
重要變數 。外部變數則關注烏俄戰爭是否停戰 、美日聯和干預
日圓與日本2028財年赤字規模 ，以及法國總統大選對歐洲政治
光譜的影響。
整體而言，2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，而是在
高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境穩健 ，
景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升級事件 ，
美元指數預期維持 97-105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓力、下有
支撐」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當通膨回落
與利率下降時美元偏弱。

## Document 374: カナダの「ＥＵ準加盟国」構想が浮上 ～欧州統合は欧州域外に広がるか？～

Source PDF: all report pdf/カナダの「ＥＵ準加盟国」構想が浮上 ～欧州統合は欧州域外に広がるか？～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年９月 17 日

1/2 
カナダの「ＥＵ準加盟国」構想が浮上 
～欧州統合は欧州域外に広がるか？～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ 欧州委員会のフォン・デア・ライエン委員長は16 日、カナダをＥＵ初の 「準加盟国」 とする
構想を表明した。米国との関係が悪化するカナダと、経済・安全保障面での対米依存低下を
目指すＥＵの思惑が重なったことが背景にある。 
○ 現行のＥＵ制度に「準加盟国」という枠組みは存在せず、構想の実現には新たな制度設計が
必要となる。単一市場への部分参加、人の移動の自由、防衛、重要鉱物など幅広い分野での
協力が想定される一方、ＥＵ法の受け入れ、司法管轄、予算拠出などの課題も多い。 
○ 実現すれば、ＥＵが英国、ウクライナ、アイスランドなどとの関係を再設計する選択肢とな
るほか、日本など欧州域外の友好国との関係強化のモデルケースとなる。ＥＵが地理的な欧
州統合から、価値観を共有する経済・安全保障圏へと変貌する契機となるかが注目される。

欧州委員会のフォン・デア・ライエン委員長は16 日の一般教書演説で、欧州歴訪中のカナダの
カーニー首相を前に、 「カナダが欧州連合（ＥＵ）の初の準加盟国となるための扉を開きたい
（opening the door for Canada being the first associate member of the EU） 」と発言した。
米国との関係が悪化するカナダは、欧州諸国との連携を深めようとしており、カーニー首相はこ
れまでもＥＵとの間で「独自の同盟関係（unique alliance） 」を構築することを提唱してきた。
加盟国政府の多くはこうした構想に慎重との報道もあるが、欧州委員会のトップ自らが踏み込ん
だ提案をしたことで、具体的な制度設計に向けた議論が始まる可能性が高まった。 
ＥＵとカナダは、2017 年から暫定適用されている「包括的経済貿易協定（CETA） 」に加えて、
2025 年には「ＥＵ・カナダ安全保障・防衛パートナーシップ」を締結し、2026 年にはＥＵの防衛
装備品の共同調達を支援する融資枠組み「欧州安全保障行動（SAFE）」に、カナダ企業が非欧州国
として初めて参加するなど、近年、関係強化を進めている。こうした枠組みを土台に、単一市場
への部分参加、非関税障壁の削減、居住・就労・就学に関する人の移動の自由、防衛協力の強
化、重要鉱物やサプライチェーン対策での連携強化などを視野に、先端製造業、テクノロジー、
人工知能（ＡＩ） 、量子、サイバー、エネルギー、需要鉱物、電池、防衛産業、北極圏など幅広い
分野での協力が構想されている。背景には、トランプ政権誕生後の米国との貿易摩擦の激化や関

### Page 2

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
2/2
係悪化を受け、ＥＵ・カナダ双方が米国への過度な依存を低下させたい思惑がある。
今回の構想はカナダのＥＵへの正式加盟を目指すものではない。ＥＵ条約49 条は、ＥＵへの加
盟資格を「欧州の国（European State） 」に限定している。また、ＥＵの現行制度に「準加盟国」
という枠組みはない。フォン・デア・ライエン委員長の発言は、既存制度へのカナダの参加を提
案したものではなく、カナダとの関係を深める新たな制度的な枠組みの創設を提唱したものと考
えられる。ＥＵにとっては、正式加盟を認めなくても、ＥＵを中心とする経済圏や安全保障圏を
拡大できるメリットがある。ＥＵ・カナダの双方にとっても、米国、中国、ロシアなど特定国へ
の依存を低下させ、価値観を共有する民主主義国との経済安全保障ネットワークを形成すること

につながる。10 月29・30 日にはカナダのモントリオールでＥＵ・カナダ首脳会議が予定されてお
り、新たな枠組みの具体化に向けてどこまで協議が進展するかに注目が集まる。
実現に向けた課題も少なくない。ＥＵはこれまで、単一市場、関税同盟、個別プログラムなど
に加盟国以外の国が参加するに当たっては、ＥＵ法の受け入れ、紛争処理時の欧州司法裁判所
（ＥＣＪ）の関与、プログラム参加費用の拠出などを求めてきた。こうした「ＥＵの意思決定に
参加できない一方、ＥＵのルールをどこまで受け入れるか」という主権を巡る問題は、英国のＥ
Ｕ離脱（ブレグジット）協議や、スイスとＥＵ間の二国間関係の見直し協議が難航する一因とな
った。また、2017 年から暫定適用されるＥＵとカナダのCETAは、ＥＵ27 ヶ国のうち批准手続きを
完了したのは17 ヶ国にとどまる。批准が進まない背景には、カナダ産農産物との競争に対する農
業関係者の懸念や、投資家と国家の紛争を扱う投資裁判制度への警戒などがある。既存の自由貿
易協定ですら完全批准に至っていない状況で、更なる関係強化ができるかどうかは不透明だ。

新たな枠組みが制度化されれば、カナダとの関係強化にとどまらず、英国、ウクライナ、アイ
スランドなどとの関係を再設計する選択肢となるほか、将来的には日本を含めた欧州域外の他国
にも対象を広げる可能性を秘めている。離脱への後悔が広がる英国では、ＥＵ関係の再強化を求
める世論も一部にあり、新たな枠組みが正式な再加盟とは異なるＥＵへの再接近の選択肢となる
可能性がある。ウクライナについてもＥＵ加盟交渉が進められているが、法の支配、汚職対策、
行政改革、ＥＵ法への適合など、幅広い改革が引き続き必要となる。また、巨大な農業国である
ウクライナの加盟は、ＥＵの共通農業政策やＥＵ予算の在り方、現加盟国の農業への影響など、
ＥＵ側にも大きな制度調整を迫る。正式加盟までの中間的な枠組みが創設されれば、より現実的
な選択肢となる可能性もある。ＥＵの共通漁業政策への参加に慎重なアイスランドでは、中断し
ているＥＵ加盟交渉を再開するか否かを問う国民投票が８月末に行われた。投票は僅差で否決さ
れたが、新たな枠組みが創設されれば将来的なＥＵ接近の選択肢の１つとなり得る。

地理的に欧州に属さないカナダを対象とする新たな「準加盟」という選択肢が加われば、ＥＵ
の統合の在り方そのものが変化する可能性がある。その場合、欧州の統合は、地理的に近接した
国の統合から、価値観や経済安全保障上の利益を共有する国々を結び付ける経済・安全保障圏へ
と発展する契機となるかもしれない。日本にとっても、ＥＵとの経済・安全保障協力をさらに深
化させる際の新たなモデルケースとして、その行方を注視する必要がある。
以 上
